| **Budget with one storage node down (fake cluster): a third of the parts become degraded, each charged `k` against the client ceiling and one against each source drive; the total drive-read volume the fakes see is 2.0 times the healthy volume, and 3.0 times the healthy load per surviving drive** | 4.3 | Degraded load hidden from the controller |
| **Deployment profiles: every file of `docs/examples/` passes validation unchanged, and each `toml` block of 10.14 and 21 headed by a file name equals that file; a process that runs both the fetch engine and the peer service serves a part whose bytes are on its own node without a network hop; `4+2` on six nodes places one shard per node and, after a node is marked `out`, rebuilds with at most two per node** | 21 | A profile that only works on one deployment; an example that drifts from the document |
| **Metadata configuration (validation only): `[metadata]` requires `hosts` (a list of `host:port`, non-empty, no repeats) and `password_file`, and `database` and `user` default to `tierstore` and `ts_rw`; `statement_timeout`, `txn_timeout`, `retry_window` and `tomb_poll` default to 100 ms, 2 s, 45 s and 2 s (a `[metadata]` with only `hosts` and `password_file` validates with those defaults, and the ordering check holds on them) and the validation of 10.14 item 9 refuses an unordered set (`statement_timeout < txn_timeout < retry_window`), a `retry_window` below 30 s, and a `tomb_poll` that is not positive or is above 60 s; for the reconciler it refuses a non-positive `reconcile.interval`, `reconcile.executor_wait` or `health.report_after`, a `txn_timeout` that is not below `lease_renew`, and a `lease` that is not above 2 x `lease_renew`; the tests exercise the ordering, the 30 s floor and the lease checks; the password is read from the file, never from an environment variable or a flag value; a voter-set key is refused as unknown; `membership.toml` is read only by `store init` and a running node ignores a changed copy; the bucket mode is refused if changed after creation** | 10.16, 10.14 | A key that silently selects the wrong store; a membership file acting as authority |
| **Components (validation only; nothing is started): all 2^9 sets of components are enumerated and each is accepted exactly when every dependency of the 10.1 table is met, and otherwise refused naming each missing one; separately, each check that is not a set dependency has a passing and a refused case (the hot store with a membership of one node and of three nodes without `[peer]`; `[fuse]` and `[nfs]` against a stated platform value); a settings section without its component is refused by name (the test enumerates every settings section, `[health]` and `[reconcile]` among the hot store's); `[client]` with both or neither of `seeds` and `membership` is refused; a `role` key in the node configuration is refused as unknown, while a membership document with one parses** | 10.1, 10.14 | A role hidden in code; a typo that silently disables a component |
| **Seed bootstrap (fake storage nodes on loopback, port 0): with three seeds of which one does not answer and one answers `404`, the client adopts the third's document; a wrong token is `401` and no other seed is tried; a document that does not validate is skipped; with no seed answering, the constructor returns `MembershipUnavailable` naming every seed, and a daemon parks reads (no miss) and retries; a response carrying a higher `x-ts-mem-epoch` makes the client fetch and adopt the newer document once, a lower epoch never moves it back, and a client with a membership file re-reads the file instead; when every known node is `down`, a seeded client returns to its seeds** | 10.8 | A client stuck on a dead address list; a client moved back to an old view |
| **Library mode (T-L): the suite of section 23, run with no daemon: client alone, cache in-process, a second process attached to a shared cache across the owner's restart, tree reads by path from the cache and straight from the client equal byte for byte; the layering check refuses each of its three negative fixtures** | 23 | A read path that only works inside the daemon; a front that bypasses the libraries |
| **NOW to LATER migration (scripted membership epochs on fake nodes): at every step every object reads correctly; nothing is placed on a `drain` hot device or drive; new PUTs go only to the three storage nodes at `replicas = 3`; every `4+2` stripe ends as `4+4` at 3/3/2 on the storage nodes with its block ids and offsets unchanged, so no index entry is rewritten; marking the combined nodes `in` again before the last step restores them as targets; the metadata members move from three combined nodes to the three storage nodes by Patroni member add and remove with the synchronous standby never absent from the set and no acknowledged commit lost; a node whose configuration disagrees with the membership's cluster settings refuses to start** | 21.3, 10.8 | A migration that needs a rewrite; data placed on a device being emptied |
| **Metadata store model in the simulator (outage, ambiguous commit, failover, rollback): the model is a linearizable Postgres whose script can make it unreachable, lose the connection during `COMMIT` with and without the commit having applied, fail over to the synchronous standby (no committed transaction lost) or to an asynchronous one (a tail of committed transactions lost), and restore an old point in time. The oracle (a model of what every acknowledged operation entails) checks that after each failover or restore: a node that sees an incarnation different from its own, or a `txn_seq` below the highest it remembers, or `frozen`, performs no unlink, stripe free, orphan sweep, trash purge, block drop, tombstone deletion or id reuse; reads, immutable-bucket PUTs and repair copies continue; and after `metadata fsck` (below) every acknowledged object is readable and no live object was deleted. A negative case that must fail: with the freeze switched off in the model, the oracle reports lost data after an asynchronous-tail failover** | 10.16, 24 | Destructive action on rolled-back metadata; silent loss after a failover |
| **Metadata fsck from footers: after the Postgres rows are wiped, or restored to an old point in time, or lose an asynchronous tail, `tierstore store metadata fsck` rebuilds blocks, hot copies, stripes and shards from `NODE` files, segment trailers, `blk/*.idx`, shard footers and large files; live counts from trailers minus applied tombstones; mutable heads from commit markers (the highest version per key whose record exists); every id allocator and every node's counter above the maximum seen; `txn_seq` above every node's remembered value; the default run is a dry run that reports each difference and writes nothing, a real run raises the incarnation and clears `frozen`, and the nodes adopt the new incarnation; an index rebuilt afterwards equals the one before the loss; a second run reports no difference; a block, stripe or shard id allocated after fsck is above every id on disk or in the rows; restore the rows to a point taken while a segment was open, with acknowledged markers only in that open segment, at a different length on each copy, and the row at epoch 1 while the copies hold a higher fence epoch: the heads are recovered and the segment later seals through the compare-and-set** | 10.16 | Id reuse after a rollback; heads or counts that disagree with the disks |
| **Fence-and-CAS seal races (deterministic simulation): see the two-sealers row; additionally, the fence is refused-below-epoch on every replica after it is recorded (a crash and restart of the replica keeps it), a segment row inserted lazily and a seal arriving first converge through the insert-or-CAS, a sealer that holds fences from fewer than `replicas - min_acks + 1` copies does not commit, and no batch under the old epoch is acknowledged after the new epoch is recorded on a replica; a copy that holds a higher epoch answers `fenced(epoch_held)` without lowering it and the sealer retries above it; an unacknowledged PUT of an immutable bucket within the sealed length is adopted (the key becomes live and `hot_unacked_records_adopted_total` counts it, even after a later DELETE), and a later duplicate with different bytes is resolved by the reconciler's lower-record-id rule** | 10.3 | A seal committed without a fence; an epoch forgotten across a restart |
| **Delete versus relocate serialization (metadata model): DELETE of K and a relocate of K's record (pack assembly, GC rewrite) run in both orders and interleaved at every statement; both lock the source block row, so one completes first; the final state is the same in all orders (K deleted, the relocated copy tombstoned or never created, live counts exact); a relocate that finds the record tombstoned writes nothing; a DELETE that finds `relocations` follows it to the new record id; a freed block's tombstone and relocation rows disappear in the transaction that frees it, and a dropped hot block keeps them until its `freed` step passes the `tomb_applied` gate; relocate, then DELETE, then a node whose entry is still at the old record id reads the key as deleted within `tomb_poll`, and again after a replay or rebuild** | 10.5, 10.16 | A relocate resurrecting a deleted key; a lost delete |
| **Mutable-bucket S3 semantics (model of Postgres, then the real server against the conformance suite): overwrite is last writer = last committed transaction, with `version` (from the cluster-wide sequence `ts.head_version`) strictly increasing across successive committed writes of a key, including across a DELETE and a re-create, gaps allowed, each commit marker carrying the version of its head row, and the previous record tombstoned in the same transaction; `If-None-Match: *` fails (412) if a live head exists and succeeds after a DELETE; `If-Match: <etag>` fails (412) unless the live head's generation equals it, on PUT and on DELETE; a failed precondition leaves the appended record garbage that is tombstoned or found by the orphan sweep and never becomes visible; DELETE answers 204 whether the key existed or not; GET and HEAD after a PUT or DELETE answer from the new head at once (read-after-write and read-after-delete) while bytes are read from any copy of the record id; LIST from `heads` equals a model for random PUT, overwrite and DELETE sequences (order, prefix, delimiter roll-up, `max-keys`, continuation, deleted keys absent, acknowledged keys present); a commit marker is written after the head transaction and a head rebuilt by fsck equals the live heads; a crash between the head transaction and its marker leaves the head authoritative** | 10.9, 10.16 | Lost update; a stale read after an overwrite; a LIST that disagrees with the heads |
| **Immutable-bucket create race detection: two writers on two membership views create one key with different bytes (every scripted interleaving of the all-node checks): both are acknowledged, the reconciler finds two live records of the key by the per-node index comparison of 24.5, keeps the lower record id, tombstones the other through Postgres, increments `store_key_conflict_total` and alerts; with identical bytes (content-derived keys) both records carry the same generation and the higher record id is tombstoned through Postgres by the same lease-checked transaction (its bytes reclaimed by the free protocol, `store_key_conflict_total` not incremented, no alert); a second live record seen by the all-node key check triggers the targeted check of its range at once; every node reaches the same answer** | 10.5, 24.5 | A silent duplicate with different bytes; nodes choosing different winners |
| **Idempotent retry after an ambiguous commit: with the connection lost during `COMMIT`, the client retries with the same operation id; if the commit applied the recorded result is returned and nothing is applied twice (a head `version` rises once; a tombstone row exists once; a stripe is freed once; a repair result is recorded once); if it did not apply the retry applies it; an `ops` row older than 24 h is purged; an update of a row whose `row_version` moved answers a conflict and is retried by the caller from a fresh read** | 10.16 | A double apply; a lost write reported as success |
| **Tombstone cursor with out-of-order commit (metadata model): two tombstone transactions take `seq` 10 and 11 and 11 commits first, with a poll between the commits; the node applies both (the later one only once the earlier transaction has finished, never skipping it), a cursor rebuilt from the node's saved horizon does not skip the later commit, and a stripe is not freed until `min(nodes.tomb_applied)` has passed both** | 10.2, 10.5, 10.16 | A cursor that was only the highest applied `seq` skipping a late commit and serving a deleted key indefinitely |
| **Durable tombstone: crash a node right after its `tomb-apply` acknowledgement, wipe or roll back Postgres to before the DELETE, run fsck: the key stays deleted (fsck reads `index/tomb-applied`); a node whose file is lost stays frozen and reported** | 10.2, 10.9, 10.16 | An acknowledged `204` undone by a restore |
| **Restore, then delete (metadata model and simulator): a point-in-time restore that moves the xid counter back, with one node down or missing the push; a key is deleted after the restore, then the stripe free is attempted; the node pulls the tombstone after it adopts the new incarnation (its cursors were reset to 0), the free waits on that node's `tomb_applied` while its `incarnation_seen` is older, and the key reads as deleted on every node; a replay mark of an older incarnation is discarded; the negative case is the same run with the cursor reset disabled, which loses the delete** | 10.2, 10.5, 10.16 | A delete lost to a rolled-back xid counter |
| **fsck floor (metadata model): with two of three nodes skipped `--apply` is refused and the incarnation and `frozen` are unchanged; with one skipped it completes and classifies no row with no files as *gone*** | 10.16 | An acknowledged delete or mutable commit dropped by a partial view |
| **Mutable PUT with no block row (metadata model): PUT a mutable key into a segment whose rows were never inserted, then GET it through another front's cold cache: the head transaction inserted the row first and the GET returns the bytes; a reader that still misses a row answers `503` with `Retry-After`, never `404` or a misdirected read** | 10.9, 10.16 | A read-after-write failure on an acknowledged write |
| **Commit markers keep their lifetime (Metadata fsck from footers): overwrite or delete all data records of the marker's segment, drop or free it, wipe Postgres, run fsck: the head is back; a relocated record whose old block is freed keeps its head through the fresh marker; a delete marker expects no resurrection; and PUT, DELETE, purge, PUT with the old marker still on disk keeps the newer write (versions come from the sequence)** | 10.2, 10.16 | A lost acknowledged write or a resurrected deleted key after fsck |
| **fsck rows with no files and bucket ids: a stripe whose files exist nowhere is classified gone and freed by `--apply` with one `fsck_rows_without_files` alert and no P0 job; a block with fewer than `k` shards is reported `lost`; mutable-bucket live counts are recomputed from the rebuilt heads; a bucket id on disk with no row is reported and bound by `--id`, and `--apply` refuses to complete while one is unbound** | 10.16, 24.12 | False `cell_lost` alarms for deleted data; records attributed to the wrong bucket |
| **Mutable sweep: a sweep that runs between the record append and the head transaction does not delete acknowledged bytes (the candidate must age `retry_window + txn_timeout` and the check locks the head row); a head transaction on an already-tombstoned record is refused and the front re-appends** | 10.5, 10.16 | An acknowledged PUT whose bytes were swept |
| **Live counts under interleaving (section 14 harness): a DELETE or overwrite interleaved with the seal in every ordering, including a delete of a record in an unsealed block: `live_count` equals records minus tombstones, never negative, never over-counted; a marker superseded while its segment is open, and a purged delete marker, each bring `live_count` to 0 and the block frees; a large file is inserted `sealed` with count 1, a DELETE before any row exists drops and unlinks it, and a large file is accepted as a pack source** | 10.16 | A stripe freed while it holds a live record |
| **Metadata client under a cancelled commit: a `COMMIT` cancelled while it waits for the standby, then a failover to the standby: the client never cancels a `COMMIT` in flight, a retry that finds the `op_id` returns the recorded result only after the sync-probe commit is acknowledged, and an `op_id` reused for another kind or subject is an error** | 10.16 | An acknowledged operation lost across a failover; a replay of another operation's result |
| **A returning node holding a hot segment or large file with no row inserts the row, keeps serving it and is not quarantined; a promoted lagging standby (`promote-lone`) freezes every node even when `txn_seq` has passed their remembered value** | 24.4, 10.16 | Acknowledged data quarantined as an orphan; a rollback hidden by other nodes' traffic |
| **503 with `Retry-After` during a metadata failover (injected clock; fake clients): while the primary is unreachable every operation that needs the metadata store (mutable-bucket PUT, GET, HEAD, DELETE and LIST; immutable-bucket DELETE; seals, destage, repair and frees queue) answers `503` with `Retry-After` after the client's retry window (`metadata.retry_window`) and never a miss, a `404` or a partial result; immutable-bucket PUT and GET and every read of a record id continue; the client reconnects to the next host on a connection error or a read-only error with jittered backoff; `tierstore-client` retries a `503` for at least 60 s honouring `Retry-After` and never treats it as absence; a failover scripted at 30 s completes the operation with no error visible to the caller, and one scripted at 90 s is reported as unavailable, not as a missing object; a fake node that stalls past the client's request timeout (library mode) is retried like a `503` with the same operation id, and the retry succeeds** | 10.9, 10.16, 23 | A failover turned into a miss or a lost write; a client giving up in 30 s |
| `digest=none` object: the record or cell crc detects a flipped cell on the serving node; the client uses the other replica or degraded read; the copy is quarantined and repaired | 9, 10.3, 10.4 | Bitrot without silo |
| S3 subset: PUT, GET range, HEAD, DELETE and LIST behave per 10.9 for both bucket modes; multipart, versioning, lifecycle, ACLs, tagging, CopyObject, object lock, server-side encryption, `PutBucket*`, presigned issuance and date preconditions on PUT return 501; GET and HEAD honour `If-Match`, `If-None-Match` (304), `If-Modified-Since` and `If-Unmodified-Since`; the CAS service's `ObjectStore` conformance runs against an immutable bucket, including "never different bytes under a used key" | 10.9 | Leaning on unsupported operations |

**Why the failure and boundary list is complete for the storage tier.** Each state a drive or node can be in (`up`, `indexing`, `stalled`, `suspect`, `down`, `failed`, `out`, full) has a row for reads and a row for writes, destage or rebuild targets; each answer a copy can give (200, 404, 412, 503 by reason, transport error, no answer) has a row in the engine; each outcome of a PUT (committed, identical, conflict, abandoned copy, generation mismatch, too few copies, not definitive, throttled, rejected) has a row in the front; each way an object can lose a copy (stalled or failed replica, node loss, NVMe loss, drive loss, bitrot, GC, hot-only eviction) has a row that shows what restores it or that it becomes a miss; each decision two nodes could make differently (a seal, a delete against a relocate, an overwrite of one key, a free against a repair) has a row under partitions and lost messages, and each state of the metadata store (up, unreachable, ambiguous commit, failover with and without a lost asynchronous tail, point-in-time rollback, frozen) has a row; each ordering between a record, a relocate and a tombstone of one key has a row, including identical bytes re-PUT after a delete; each crash point of destage, conversion, rebuild and recovery has a scripted row; each boundary of a size rule (`large_min`, `block_bytes - block_reserve`, `seal_min_bytes`, the marks, `fill_limit` and the rebuild ceiling) has a row on both sides; and an object that is lost beyond the code is a miss, never wrong bytes. On the client side of revision 8: each component set is enumerated and either starts or is refused with its missing dependencies; each answer a seed can give (none, `404`, `401`, an invalid document, a valid one) and each epoch relation (higher, equal, lower) has a row.

## 15. How constants are frozen
Starting constants in sections 4, 5 and 10 are replaced by T0, T0-F, T0-S and T10b reports; a constant without a measurement behind it is marked [I] in the code comment that defines it.

## 16. Farm measurement plan (T0, T0-F and T0-S, before tuning)
Farm targets, never on a developer machine. On a build node, run on **one node drained of jobs** (no mounts, no Prepare) while the jobscheduler holds admission on the others; on a storage node, run before it holds data, or on drives that carry no data (scratch files go in `ts-scratch` directories on the drives' xfs, never in `ts-hot` or `ts-cold` of a live drive, never on silo's data). Before each run, record `iostat -x` of the drives involved for 60 s and abort if util > 5%. Each cell: 20 s after a 5 s warm-up, page cache dropped between cells; output MB/s, p50, p99, `iostat -x` (`rareq-sz`, `r_await`, `aqu-sz`, `util`). **Nothing here loads a kernel module or changes firewall or socket state on a node** (C-13 runs on a deployed metadata store, 10.16, where the watchdog is part of the deployment).

**A. Raw drives (T0-S, on the storage nodes once they exist; one HDD, then all 15 at once; the NVMe).**
- *Facts, read only:* model, rpm, `lsblk -t -D -z` (zoned or not), `smartctl -i`, `xfs_info`, SCSI `timeout` and `eh_timeout`, scheduler, `nr_requests`, write-cache state; **CMR or SMR**, which decides whether the steady-state churn arm below runs in full.
- *HDD sequential:* request size {1, 2, 4, 8, 16 MiB} x streams {1, 2, 4, 8} x {buffered readahead, `O_DIRECT`} x {adjacent, scattered}; the same with all 15 drives busy (HBA and backplane ceilings). Decides `pos_ms`, `media_MBps`, `gap_cells`, and whether the cold tier uses `O_DIRECT` or buffered.
- *NVMe:* power-loss protection (the owner states the LATER drives have it; T0-S verifies it from the vendor data and `nvme id-ctrl`, read only, and the hardware audit refuses a drive found without it; group commit, `hot.min_acks`, `batch_max` and Postgres's default `synchronous_commit = on` are sized assuming it, and nothing is silently retuned for a drive without it); sequential write MB/s; `fdatasync` latency distribution for 1 KiB to 1 MiB appends at 1, 4 and 16 writers, on an appended file, a `fallocate`d file and a pre-written file (group-commit batch sizes and the latency a small PUT sees); read latency p50 and p99 at queue depths {1, 4, 16, 64}; mixed 70/30; buffered versus `O_DIRECT` reads; `fadvise(DONTNEED)` effect. Also, on the metadata partition of the NVMe (a separate xfs partition, 10.16): `fdatasync` latency at 1 writer for 8 KiB appends (the WAL pattern) and for etcd's write-ahead log. Decides `hot.queue_depth`, `batch_max`, the read path.
- *Network:* link speed (`ethtool`, read only) and a Rust bench target for throughput and latency storage-to-storage, build-to-storage, and 31 build nodes pulling at once (the fan-in), with and without erasure-coding CPU load.
- *CPU:* `reed-solomon-simd` encode and decode MB/s per core for `4+2`, `4+4`, `5+4`, `6+3` at 1, 2 and 4 MiB windows and 1 GiB blocks, including a parity-shard rebuild (restore data, re-encode); blake3 and crc32c MB/s; `sha256` MB/s per core with `sha_ni` on the build nodes. Decides encode threads, hash threads and `cpu.weight`.

**B. Cache device (build node).** On (i) the loop path (the first results are in the measured facts: `O_DIRECT` absorbed by the page cache, buffered writers stalled for more than 20 s), (ii) a file on the host root fs, (iii) the new partition once it exists: write block {128 KiB, 1 MiB, 4 MiB} x jobs {1, 4, 16} x {buffered + `fdatasync` per 32 MiB, `O_DIRECT`}; `fdatasync` latency at 1 MiB batched every 1 s; 70% 128 KiB random reads over 30% sequential writes; `PUNCH_HOLE` on 16 MiB groups and whether free space returns; DONTNEED arms (a) never, (b) at commit, (c) cap-driven, on a read-after-serve pattern. **Records `findmnt` of the root fs.** Records the partition's numbers for section 11 and decides the dirty cap, hash pool and DONTNEED default.

**C. The storage tier.** C-1, C-6, C-7 and C-10 need no tier code and run first (T0-F-a); the others run on the tier's own code (T0-F-b, T0-S).
- **C-1 silo baseline (legacy farm):** ranged GETs through the local silo, part size {4, 8, 16, 32, 64} MiB x concurrent GETs per node {1, 2, 4, 8} x active nodes {1, 6}; per-drive `iostat` (`rareq-sz`, kB read per GET byte, util).
- **C-2 one HDD streaming whole packs (storage node, prototype or tier code):** whole-pack and 64 MiB part reads, streams per drive {1, 2, 3, 4}, chunk {1, 2, 4, 8} MiB, depth {1, 2, 4}, slice {8..128} MiB, `O_DIRECT` versus buffered `sendfile` with DONTNEED; MB/s per drive, bytes per busy-second, p50 and p99 of a **1 MiB and a 4 MiB demand read** arriving at an already-busy drive with and without demand preemption.
- **C-2b mixed read and write on one HDD:** one shard-write stream and one read stream with demand arrivals at {0, 2, 8} per second, at `write_min_share` {0, 25, 50}%; decides `write_min_share` and confirms the class order.
- **C-3 hot versus cold from many clients:** 1, 6 and 31 build nodes fetching packs of {16 MiB, 256 MiB, 1 GiB, 4 GiB} from (i) the hot tier (either replica) and (ii) the cold tier (one shard); hedge to the other replica against a loaded drive; striping across hot replicas for packs over 128 MiB (4.4); network CPU. **C-3b promote:** a skewed read mix with and without promotion; hit rate, promo bytes, NVMe cost.
- **C-4 idle and loaded first byte:** GET of {1, 2, 4, 8} MiB at cluster concurrency 1 (decides the first-part size: smallest with idle first byte <= 40 ms for cold), and the same under the C-3 load (the thresholds of 4.6).
- **C-5 write path:** hot PUT latency and rate for 1 KiB, 64 KiB, 1 MiB records and 4 MiB to 1 GiB files at replicas 1 and 2 (the ack rule, group-commit batch distribution, one slow replica, seal-and-roll time); cold shard writes (1 GiB sequential, `O_DIRECT` 4 MiB depth 2, with and without the 64 MiB extent hint and `fallocate`; extent count per file; allocated bytes against file size) at 1 and 2 writers per drive; the whole destage pipeline for `4+2`, `4+4`, `replicas = 2` (stripes per hour, CPU, network); sustained write MB/s over 200 GiB per drive.
- **C-5b steady-state churn (only if C0 finds SMR; otherwise a short check):** a drive filled to 85%, then stripes freed by `unlink` and new shards written into the holes for **at least 3 drive capacities of writes**, with a read stream beside it; sustained write MB/s per drive capacity written, the ratio to a fresh drive, read p99, extent counts over time.
- **C-6 workload facts:** the object-size distribution of silo's objects split by kind (packs, tree lists, L2 sidecars, other): count and bytes per bucket {<= 64 KiB, <= 1 MiB, <= 4 MiB, <= 16 MiB, <= 256 MiB, larger}; `L_silo` and `L_live`; the explanation of silo's raw-to-logical gap; the identity of the requesters behind today's GETs and of the 147 DELETEs. Decides `large_min`, `block_bytes` and `destage.pack_window` (from the pack size distribution: first-fit waste for 400 to 700 MiB packs), confirms the capacity table (10.15) and the import volume (10.10).
- **C-7 placement:** distribution of the real key set under node rank; shard placement over 45 drives with zero, one and two nodes down (offline computation).
- **C-8 slow consumer:** two clients that stop reading mid-stream and a third that reads normally, against one HDD, with slot release on and off. Decides `slot_release_after`.
- **C-9 failing drive and startup:** read the kernel's per-device command and error-handling timeouts on every drive, and run the tier's injected hang (a test I/O layer that never completes a chunk) on real threads; a real failing-drive injection only if it can be induced without loading a module or touching a drive with data, otherwise reported as not run. **Index recovery:** reopen of the embedded store from its replay mark, open-segment replay (a 1 GiB segment of 1 KiB records), trailer reads of hot blocks, `blk/*.idx` load, and the cold-trailer rebuild (one read per block on a drive of 5.6 k shards, cold dentry cache), at 1.4 M and at a synthetic 100 M keys; bytes per key on disk and in cache, point-read p99 for `LOCATE`, bulk-load time, for `fjall` and `redb`.
- **C-10 GC replay (offline):** replay the death times of silo's packs (from the CAS service's data once its GC, or a synthetic min_ttl 7 d / action_ttl 30 d, has run) against (a) arrival-order stripes and (b) expiry-class stripes; report stripes freed whole, live fraction of the rest, rewrite bytes per byte freed at `rewrite_below` {0.2, 0.3, 0.5}, and `cold_fill_efficiency` over time (sealed block sizes at today's ingest with the age-seal floor, partial stripes, dead bytes).
- **C-11 erasure coding in operation:** aggregate cold read MB/s from 31 clients with one node down (the 2.8 GB/s estimate of 4.3); degraded read latency for ranges {4 KiB, 64 KiB, 1 MiB, 4 MiB} with one drive down and with one node down (`k` reads from `k` drives versus the healthy read), hedged and not; rebuild of a scratch drive (200 GiB of shards) at `rebuild.share` {10, 25, 50, 100}% with a foreground read load: rebuild MB/s, foreground p99; scrub rate and its foreground cost.
- **C-12 failure drills:** a storage node taken down mid-PUT-storm and mid-fill; a drive stalled by the injected hang; seal-and-roll under a slow replica; node return after `< node_down_after` (nothing rebuilt) and a node declared `out` (rebuild onto two nodes at `4+4`).
- **C-13 metadata store (T-S22, T-S23; on both profiles):** on LATER, the three storage nodes with Postgres on its NVMe partition; on NOW, three of the six combined nodes with Postgres on a 64 GiB partition of the SATA SSD (no power-loss protection, honest `fsync`, load average above 100 on 88 CPUs). Each run uses the real Patroni, etcd and Postgres packages of T-S23 and the `tierstore-meta` client. Measure: **commit latency** (p50, p99, max) of the hot-path transactions (the mutable-bucket head upsert, an immutable DELETE with tombstones, a block seal compare-and-set, a descriptor commit) at 1, 8 and 64 concurrent clients with `synchronous_commit = on` and a synchronous standby, on both profiles (the figures the design assumes are labelled [I] and are those of 10.16: a whole synchronous-commit round trip of about 1 ms on LATER and a few ms on NOW, a single WAL flush of tens of microseconds on LATER and milliseconds on NOW); point read of a head, upsert and prefix LIST (range scan with delimiter roll-up) over `heads` at 1e6, 1e7 and 1e8 rows (size on disk, vacuum behaviour, which states or confirms the bound of about 1e8 mutable keys); `tombstones` and `ops` growth and vacuum under a delete storm; **failover time** (kill the primary process, power-cycle the primary's node, power-cycle the node that hosts both the primary and the etcd leader, partition the primary from etcd, planned switchover) from the failure to the first committed transaction through the client, at least 10 runs each, p50 and max, against the target of at most 30 s, with `ttl`, `loop_wait`, `retry_timeout` and, on NOW, etcd's heartbeat and election timeouts (250 ms and 2.5 s) as the variables; the client's `503` window as the callers see it; replica re-initialisation time (`pg_basebackup`) for a full partition; the load of Patroni, etcd and Postgres on a loaded NOW node with and without a raised `CPUWeight`. The measurement itself loads no module and changes no firewall state: the watchdog device is part of the deployment of T-S23, not of the measurement.
- Per run: aggregate MB/s, per-request p50/p99, first byte, per-drive iostat, CPU.
- Decides: **P**, `s*`, `per_drive_max`, chunk, slice, depth, `demand_preempt_max`, `write_min_share`, `slot_release_after`, `stall_after`, node floor, first-part size, thresholds in 4.6, `large_min`, `seal_max_age`, the marks and `destage` constants, `promote` constants, `rebuild.share`, the fill marks, the code default against capacity, striping default, and, from C-13, `metadata.statement_timeout`, `metadata.txn_timeout`, `metadata.retry_window`, the Patroni and etcd timings, and the mutable-bucket key bound.

**D. CPU.** In A. **E. Node facts.** On every build node: `uname -r`, `fuse.enable_uring`, fuse module state, CPU count, `kernel.io_uring_disabled`, memlock limits, seccomp profile of job sandboxes, the FUSE request timeout, `vm.dirty_*`; **root filesystem type** and free unallocated SSD space; and, read only, **how the tree id is derived (T-FMT Q1)**. On every storage node: CPU model and count, RAM, NUMA, NIC speed, HBA and drive topology. Decides which build nodes run io_uring, the cache device plan, and the encode thread counts.

**Acceptance of T0, T0-F and T0-S (report, not code):** one table per section, the final constants, and any contradicted inference named as such.

## 17. Benchmark plan
The benchmarks below compare candidate FUSE libraries (fuser, fuse3/rfuse3, polyfuse, fuse-backend-rs) with `tsfuse`, and the storage tier with silo. Results are published with their raw data and configuration. Numbers come from **one farm node**, one harness throughout.

### 17.1 FUSE layer
**Harness.** `ts-fs-open-stall-load`: N open/read/close processes over a real komira tree (same tree and file list for every variant) plus CPU burners (one per core), 60 s per run, **at least 5 runs per variant, median and min/max**, first run discarded. Node drained. Per run record kernel, `fuse` module parameters, CPU governor and count, mount options, `entry_timeout`/`attr_timeout`, `max_read`/`max_write`, open mode, reader count, library commit. Harness and raw data live in a bench crate; no shell.

**Variants.** V1 fuser fork as shipped in 0.3.119 (baseline 2,769 opens/s, p50 74 ms, p99 312 ms; re-run on the day). V2 fork plus the pin fix of komira-ai/tierstore#343 (28,199 opens/s, p50 7.7 ms, p99 26 ms; re-run). V3 `tsfuse` `/dev/fuse`, 1 reader and N cloned readers (N in {2, 4, 8, cores/4}), writev replies. V4 same with splice replies. V5 `tsfuse` over io_uring, default depth and larger. V6 (optional, only if each costs under a day) a minimal read-only filesystem on fuse3 (rfuse3) and fuse-backend-rs over the same mapped list; polyfuse and the rest are a **static feature table** (async dispatch, clone, splice, uring, resend, copy count per read) read from source at pinned commits; unread cells stay empty.

**Two regimes, both reported.** (a) Library regime: `entry_timeout=0`, `attr_timeout=0`, explicit OPEN each time (the shipped baselines' settings). (b) Production regime: long timeouts and `NO_OPEN`. A **null filesystem** isolates transport from tierstore logic; V3-V5 run with both.

**Metrics.** opens/s; GETATTR and OPEN latency p50, p99, max (library histograms and client side); read MB/s cold and warm; **CPU per 1k requests** (server `utime+stime`, cgroup kernel time, whole-node delta); **D-state and FUSE waiting under a real komira build** (named targets): `fuse_d_state_threads`, `/sys/fs/fuse/connections/<n>/waiting`, D-state counts, per-request latency histogram, build wall time and FUSE-wait fraction; for V5 `uring_entries_parked` and `uring_registered_bytes` under a cold-read storm of `depth x queues x 1.5` and metadata p99 during it.

**Decision rules (before the data).** io_uring is default only if it improves CPU per 1k requests or p99 by at least 20% against `/dev/fuse` with clones, does not regress cold or warm read MB/s by more than 5%, survives the storm without metadata p99 growth, **fits 64 MiB of registered memory per mount**, and satisfies the takeover requirement (5.2 item 5). Splice replaces `pread`+`writev` above a threshold only where it wins on CPU per 1k requests at equal MB/s. DONTNEED stays on only if warm MB/s does not fall and page cache falls. The number of cloned readers is the knee of opens/s versus CPU; the shared pending queue (5.2) predicts a knee.

### 17.2 The storage tier versus silo (and a reference run)
Same harness, 5 runs, one table (T-B5). Silo measured on its own farm (C-1) and the storage tier on the storage nodes; where a like-for-like drive comparison is needed, the same HDD model is used or the difference is stated.
1. **One HDD streaming whole packs** (tier, 1, 2, 3 streams) versus **silo 8+4 ranged GETs** (8, 16, 32, 64 MiB at 1, 2, 4, 8 concurrent): MB/s per drive, bytes per busy-second, kB read per GET byte. **Hot tier** reads of the same packs from NVMe.
2. **Tail:** p50/p99/max of 1 MiB and 4 MiB demand reads under a background of {0, 2, 6} streams per drive: silo (waits for the slowest of 8 queues) versus the tier (one queue, preemption, hedge to the second hot replica or a degraded read).
3. **Pack fill end to end:** time to fill a 1 GiB and a 4 GiB pack into the node cache and tree-ready time for a real komira tree, at 1, 6 and 31 build nodes at once, from hot and from cold.
4. **Write:** 64 MiB and 1 GiB pack PUT at `hot.replicas` 1 and 2 against silo PUT; small-object PUTs at the measured size distribution (rate and p99); the destage rate; the cold sustained-write curve fresh and in steady-state churn (C-5b).
5. **Failure drills (reported here; no timing asserted in tests):** **one storage node down under a 31-node cold-start read storm, with a pass bar fixed before the run: aggregate cold read at least 2.5 GB/s (90% of the 2.8 GB/s estimate) and demand first-byte p99 at most 3 times the healthy p99 at the same load; a miss of the bar is reported and reopens the budget of 4.3 before T10b freezes constants**; a storage node down mid-fill; a drive stalled by the injected hang; degraded-read p99; rebuild time of a scratch drive and the extrapolation to a full one; fraction of keys with margin 0 and the audit histograms before and after.
6. **Reference run (optional, bounded to two days of a farm slot):** a single-node `weed server -s3` conformance run (create-only under concurrency, content ETag, ranged GET, prefix LIST, behaviour with the lock service down) and a two-node `replication 001` whole-pack read with and without vacuum on two idle storage nodes, replaying about a day of the measured PUT and GET mix with 1 GiB packs. It is a reference for 10.12, not a gate; it also validates the fallback of 10.12.

Raw CSVs and exact configuration are published with the result tables.

### 17.3 Does Prepare's order matter (T10b)
T10b reports, per build target over its jobs, the **first-touch hit rate** (`first_touch_total{ready="yes"}` over all first touches: a first read of a cell in a job session that found it `present`) and the **time to first read** (`first_read_seconds` p50 and p99), with Prepare's order of 6.1, beside the job's `read_wait_seconds`, `launch_before_ready_total` and amplification. These numbers decide whether the publish-time order hint of 6.1 is worth building: it is considered only if a material share of jobs waits on first touches that Prepare had not fetched yet. No test asserts any of these numbers (the measurement is a report).

## 18. Work breakdown (PR-sized, parallel, with interfaces, tests and start points)
**Status of the storage-tier tasks (owner, 2026-11-18).** The agents now focus on the client side, SeaweedFS is trialled as the store, and the implementation of our own store is paused. **The storage-tier tasks (T-S0 to T-S25, the store items of T-B5, and any task that only serves our store: T-SB, which reads our store's membership route; T-S11 is not needed and T11b is done) are not dispatched; of T-I2 (the freeze delta), the store-side items (the `Meta` trait, `fence`, `tomb-apply`, the format amendments) are paused and the client-side items (`Role` removal, `MEMBERSHIP` route's client, `PACK_SHARED`, load in `LOCATE`) continue.** T-K1b writes packs to a store's S3 front and tolerates `503` for 60 s; its target is whichever store the trial settles on, and this document does not dispatch it. They remain, unchanged in substance, as the plan of record if the owner resumes the store, and nothing in them is in progress: no pull request of them is open and none is assumed to be running. Where this section says what a store task depends on, when it could start or how long its chain is, that is the order and the estimate **if the store is resumed**, not a schedule. **The client-side tasks continue** (T0, T-F0 to T-F9, T-ENV, T-FMT, T-K1a, T-K1c, T-J1, T-L, T1 to T14, T-M1, T-M2, T-B1 to T-B4, T-R, T11a, T10b) and are written for any store behind the peer protocol and the S3 API (10.17 ends with this). The merged code of the earlier store drafts stays in the repository as it is until a resumed T-I2 amends it (the list under the table); it now includes the `tierstore-index` crate of T-S3, merged in revision 8 form as komira-ai/tierstore#367.
Each task below is one or a few pull requests. **Starts** says when a task can begin (for a paused storage-tier task, when it could begin if the store is resumed): **PR up** means the moment this design is up for review, with no wait for any other task; "after X" names the tasks whose interfaces (on fakes) or results it needs. Each task's tests are the section 14 rows it makes pass (T10a holds the shared fakes), and each PR names them (rule T8).
Day-0 freeze: trait interfaces, the state page layout, the wire protocol, the store's on-disk and on-wire formats and the peer protocol land first with in-tree fakes, so every other task runs in parallel against fakes. The store's traits are frozen with the rules of section 10 already in them: content-derived generation, `written_ms` in the record (informational, never an order), the commit marker of mutable buckets, relocate and tombstone formats (a tombstone names a record id) and the liveness rule, the record, trailer, block, footer and stripe-descriptor formats, create-only by the index under a key lock with the all-node check for immutable buckets (`Index`, `HotStore`); the replication epoch, the fence and the ack rule (`HotStore`); the metadata store's operations as a trait with an in-tree model (`Meta`, implemented by `tierstore-meta` and by the fake: transactions keyed by operation id and row version, the incarnation check, `txn_seq`); the placement invariant and stripe descriptors (`Placement`, `ColdStore`); watchdog, scheduler liveness, read-ahead and slot release, write share, small lane, threads = streams x depth (`DriveSched`); `LOCATE`, per-copy `If-Match` and locations that carry generations (`Upstream`, `PeerClient`).
